<div class="tdb-block-inner td-fix-index"><h1 class="tdb-title-text">The Four Models of Public Relations</h1><div></div><div class="tdb-title-line"></div></div></div><div class="td_block_wrap tdb_single_subtitle tdi_55 td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_55" >

<div class="tdb-block-inner td-fix-index"><h2>Four models to unlock PR success.</h2></div></div><div class="td_block_wrap tdb_single_author tdi_56 td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1 tdb-post-meta"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_56" >

<div class="wpb_wrapper" ><div class="td_block_wrap td_block_text_with_title tdi_62 tagdiv-type td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_62" >

<div class="td-block-title-wrap"></div><div class="td_mod_wrap td-fix-index"><p style="text-align: right;">Cover photo: <a href="https://www.instagram.com/jerrysilfwer/">@jerrysilfwer</a></p>
</div></div></div></div></div></div><div id="tdi_63" class="tdc-row stretch_row_1400 td-stretch-content"><div class="vc_row tdi_64 td-ss-row td-scroll-content wpb_row td-pb-row tdc-element-style" >

<div class="tdi_63_rand_style td-element-style" ><div class="td-element-style-before"></div></div><div class="vc_column tdi_66  wpb_column vc_column_container tdc-column td-pb-span3">
<div class="wpb_wrapper" ></div></div><div class="vc_column tdi_68  wpb_column vc_column_container tdc-column td-pb-span6">
<div class="wpb_wrapper" ><div class="td_block_wrap tdb_single_custom_field tdi_69 tdb-sacff-type-text td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_69" >

<div class="tdb-block-inner td-fix-index"><div class="tdb-cf-add-txt">tl:dr;</div><div class="tdb-sacff-txt">Grunig and Hunt’s four models of <a alt="What is Public Relations?" href="https://doctorspin.net/what-is-public-relations/">public relations</a> provide valuable insights into the evolution of communication strategies and their implications for modern businesses.</div></div></div> <!-- ./block --><div class="td_block_wrap tdb_single_toc tdi_73 td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_73" >


<div class="tdi_73_rand_style td-element-style"><style></style></div><div class="tdb-block-inner td-fix-index"><h3 class="tdb-stoc-title">Table of contents <span id="flip">[hide]</span></h3> <div id="panel"><ul><li><a href="#the-four-models-of-public-relations">The Four Models of Public Relations</a></li><li><a href="#model-1-press-agentry-model">Model 1: Press Agentry Model</a></li><li><a href="#model-2-public-information-model">Model 2: Public Information Model</a></li><li><a href="#model-3-two-way-asymmetrical-model">Model 3: Two-Way Asymmetrical Model</a></li><li><a href="#model-4-two-way-symmetrical-model">Model 4: Two-Way Symmetrical Model</a></li><li><a href="#pr-models-for-every-situation">PR Models for Every Situation</a></li><li><a href="#an-important-framework-for-pr">An Important Framework for&nbsp;PR</a></li><li><a href="#pr-resource-introduction-pr-course">PR Resource: Introduction PR Course</a></li><li><a href="#spin-s-pr-school-free-introduction-pr-course">Spin’s PR School: Free Introduction PR Course</a></li></ul></div></div></div><div class="td_block_wrap tdb_single_content tdi_74 td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1 td-post-content tagdiv-type"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_74" >
<div class="tdb-block-inner td-fix-index">
<p data-block-type="core"><strong>There are four mod­els of pub­lic relations.</strong></p>



<p data-block-type="core">In their sem­in­al work, “<a href="https://www.researchgate.net/profile/James-Grunig/publication/322802009_Managing_Public_Relations/links/5a70b327a6fdcc33daa9dfad/Managing-Public-Relations.pdf" rel="noopener">Managing Public Relations</a>,” James Grunig and Todd Hunt (1984) intro­duced four mod­els of pub­lic rela­tions that have since become the most widely cited in aca­dem­ic circles.&nbsp;</p>



<p data-block-type="core">These four PR mod­els — press agentry, pub­lic inform­a­tion, two-way asym­met­ric­al, and two-way sym­met­ric­al — are essen­tial build­ing blocks for craft­ing com­pre­hens­ive com­mu­nic­a­tion strategies that address an organ­isa­tion’s diverse needs.</p>



<p data-block-type="core">Here we&nbsp;go:</p>



<h2  id="the-four-models-of-public-relations" class="wp-block-heading" data-block-type="core">The Four Models of Public Relations</h2>



<p data-block-type="core">In the Excellence study, <a href="https://kdpaine.blogs.com/files/encylopedia-of-communication-9781405131995_chapter_399.pdf" rel="noopener">James Grunig and Todd Hunt (1984)</a> developed the most widely cited <a href="https://books.google.se/books?hl=en&amp;lr=&amp;id=RZKMAQAAQBAJ&amp;oi=fnd&amp;pg=PP1&amp;dq=grunig+excellence+study&amp;ots=sfgatlGlJW&amp;sig=csiSAVCGlpqjfH84vu3dVeu_qLg&amp;redir_esc=y#v=onepage&amp;q=grunig%20excellence%20study&amp;f=false" rel="noopener">PR mod­el</a> in aca­dem­ic circles.&nbsp;</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full td-caption-align-center" data-block-type="core"><picture decoding="async" class="wp-image-75117" title="Four Models of Public Relations | PR Theories | Doctor Spin">
<source type="image/avif" data-lazy-srcset="https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog.jpeg.avif 1920w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-750x422.jpeg.avif 750w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-140x80.jpeg.avif 140w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-768x432.jpeg.avif 768w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-747x420.jpeg.avif 747w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-150x84.jpeg.avif 150w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-300x169.jpeg.avif 300w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-696x392.jpeg.avif 696w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-1068x601.jpeg.avif 1068w" sizes="(max-width: 1920px) 100vw, 1920px"/>
<img decoding="async" width="1920" height="1080" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201920%201080'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Four Models of Public Relations - Grunig - The PR Blog" data-lazy-srcset="https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog.jpeg 1920w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-750x422.jpeg 750w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-1024x576.jpeg 1024w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-140x80.jpeg 140w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-768x432.jpeg 768w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-1536x864.jpeg 1536w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-747x420.jpeg 747w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-150x84.jpeg 150w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-300x169.jpeg 300w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-696x392.jpeg 696w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-1068x601.jpeg 1068w" data-lazy-sizes="(max-width: 1920px) 100vw, 1920px" data-lazy-src="https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog.jpeg"/><noscript><img decoding="async" width="1920" height="1080" src="https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog.jpeg" alt="Four Models of Public Relations - Grunig - The PR Blog" srcset="https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog.jpeg 1920w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-750x422.jpeg 750w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-1024x576.jpeg 1024w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-140x80.jpeg 140w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-768x432.jpeg 768w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-1536x864.jpeg 1536w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-747x420.jpeg 747w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-150x84.jpeg 150w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-300x169.jpeg 300w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-696x392.jpeg 696w, https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/Four-Models-of-Public-Relations-Grunig-The-PR-Blog-1068x601.jpeg 1068w" sizes="(max-width: 1920px) 100vw, 1920px"/></noscript>
</picture>
<figcaption class="wp-element-caption">The four mod­els of pub­lic relations.</figcaption></figure>



<p data-block-type="core">It’s not one mod­el but <em>four</em> in sequence.</p>



<p data-block-type="core">Grunig and Hunt’s four mod­els of pub­lic rela­tions provide valu­able insights into the evol­u­tion of com­mu­nic­a­tion strategies and their implic­a­tions for mod­ern businesses.</p>



<div class="wp-block-stackable-icon-list stk-block-icon-list stk-block stk-isl2fje" data-block-id="isl2fje"><style></style><svg style="display:none"><defs><g id="stk-icon-list__icon-svg-def-isl2fje"><svg viewBox="0 0 512 512"><path d="M256 8C119 8 8 119 8 256s111 248 248 248 248-111 248-248S393 8 256 8zm0 448c-110.5 0-200-89.5-200-200S145.5 56 256 56s200 89.5 200 200-89.5 200-200 200zm101.8-262.2L295.6 256l62.2 62.2c4.7 4.7 4.7 12.3 0 17l-22.6 22.6c-4.7 4.7-12.3 4.7-17 0L256 295.6l-62.2 62.2c-4.7 4.7-12.3 4.7-17 0l-22.6-22.6c-4.7-4.7-4.7-12.3 0-17l62.2-62.2-62.2-62.2c-4.7-4.7-4.7-12.3 0-17l22.6-22.6c4.7-4.7 12.3-4.7 17 0l62.2 62.2 62.2-62.2c4.7-4.7 12.3-4.7 17 0l22.6 22.6c4.7 4.7 4.7 12.3 0 17z" /></svg></g></defs></svg><ul class="stk-block-icon-list__ul stk-block-icon-list--column">
<li class="wp-block-stackable-icon-list-item stk-block-icon-list-item stk-block stk-gwyha8o" data-block-id="gwyha8o"><div class="stk-block-icon-list-item__content"><span class="stk--svg-wrapper"><div class="stk--inner-svg"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="32" height="32"><use xlink:href="#stk-icon-list__icon-svg-def-isl2fje" xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink" /></svg></div></span><span class="stk-block-icon-list-item__text"><strong>PR mod­el 1: Press Agentry Model</strong>. The organ­isa­tion uses <a alt="Free Media PR Course" href="https://doctorspin.net/free-media-pr-course/">media</a> manip­u­la­tion to shape the nar­rat­ive deceptively.</span></div></li>



<li class="wp-block-stackable-icon-list-item stk-block-icon-list-item stk-block stk-r8t71ki" data-block-id="r8t71ki"><div class="stk-block-icon-list-item__content"><span class="stk--svg-wrapper"><div class="stk--inner-svg"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="32" height="32"><use xlink:href="#stk-icon-list__icon-svg-def-isl2fje" xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink" /></svg></div></span><span class="stk-block-icon-list-item__text"><strong>PR mod­el 2: Public Information Model</strong>. The organ­isa­tion is prac­tising one-way com­mu­nic­a­tion to dis­sem­in­ate inform­a­tion with little or no feed­back from recipients.</span></div></li>



<li class="wp-block-stackable-icon-list-item stk-block-icon-list-item stk-block stk-f6n8r01" data-block-id="f6n8r01"><div class="stk-block-icon-list-item__content"><span class="stk--svg-wrapper"><div class="stk--inner-svg"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="32" height="32"><use xlink:href="#stk-icon-list__icon-svg-def-isl2fje" xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink" /></svg></div></span><span class="stk-block-icon-list-item__text"><strong>PR mod­el 3: Two-Way Asymmetrical Model</strong>. The organ­isa­tion engages in two-way com­mu­nic­a­tion to per­suade and estab­lish power structures.</span></div></li>



<li class="wp-block-stackable-icon-list-item stk-block-icon-list-item stk-block stk-mo9qvsr" data-block-id="mo9qvsr"><div class="stk-block-icon-list-item__content"><span class="stk--svg-wrapper"><div class="stk--inner-svg"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="32" height="32"><use xlink:href="#stk-icon-list__icon-svg-def-isl2fje" xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink" /></svg></div></span><span class="stk-block-icon-list-item__text"><strong>PR mod­el 4: Two-Way Symmetrical Model</strong>. The organ­isa­tion engages in two-way com­mu­nic­a­tion to find com­mon ground and mutu­al benefits.</span></div></li>
</ul></div>



<p data-block-type="core">The four mod­els of pub­lic rela­tions as a&nbsp;frame­work can be con­sidered a&nbsp;corner­stone of the Excellence Approach. <span class="footnote_referrer"><span role="button" tabindex="0" onclick="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_1');" onkeypress="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_1');"><sup id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_65597_2_1" class="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text">1</sup></span><span id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text_65597_2_1" class="footnote_tooltip">Silfwer, J. (2022, November 6).&nbsp;3&nbsp;PR Approaches: Excellence, Rhetorical, and Critical. Doctor Spin | The PR Blog. <a href="https://doctorspin.net/pr-approaches/"><span class="footnote_url_wrap">https://​doc​tor​spin​.net/​p​r​-​a​p​p​r​o​a​c​h​es/</span></a></span></span><script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript" data-rocket-type="text/javascript"> jQuery('#footnote_plugin_tooltip_65597_2_1').tooltip({ tip: '#footnote_plugin_tooltip_text_65597_2_1', tipClass: 'footnote_tooltip', effect: 'fade', predelay: 0, fadeInSpeed: 200, delay: 400, fadeOutSpeed: 200, position: 'top center', relative: true, offset: [-7, 0], });</script></p>



<p class="has-seasalt-background-color has-background" data-block-type="core"><strong>The excel­lence PR approach</strong> = this pub­lic rela­tions approach focuses on object­ives and cor­por­ate value cre­ation. The under­ly­ing motiv­a­tion behind the the­ory was that pub­lic rela­tions were mainly a&nbsp;vari­ety of tac­tic­al tools that des­per­ately needed a&nbsp;man­age­ment the­ory to work well in a&nbsp;soph­ist­ic­ated organ­isa­tion. <span class="footnote_referrer"><span role="button" tabindex="0" onclick="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_2');" onkeypress="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_2');"><sup id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_65597_2_2" class="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text">2</sup></span><span id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text_65597_2_2" class="footnote_tooltip">Silfwer, J. (2022, November 6).&nbsp;PR Approaches: Excellence, Rhetorical, and Critical. Doctor Spin | The PR Blog. <a href="https://doctorspin.net/pr-approaches/"><span class="footnote_url_wrap">https://​doc​tor​spin​.net/​p​r​-​a​p​p​r​o​a​c​h​es/</span></a></span></span><script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript" data-rocket-type="text/javascript"> jQuery('#footnote_plugin_tooltip_65597_2_2').tooltip({ tip: '#footnote_plugin_tooltip_text_65597_2_2', tipClass: 'footnote_tooltip', effect: 'fade', predelay: 0, fadeInSpeed: 200, delay: 400, fadeOutSpeed: 200, position: 'top center', relative: true, offset: [-7, 0], });</script><br><br><em>Notable men­tions: <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_E._Grunig" rel="noopener">James E. Grunig</a>, <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larissa_Grunig" rel="noopener">Larissa A. Grunig</a></em></p>



<p data-block-type="core">The res­ult? The research­ers con­cluded that the two-way sym­met­ric­al mod­el is the most effect­ive way to prac­tice pub­lic relations.</p>



<p class="has-background" style="background-color:#fcfaf9" data-block-type="core"><span class="dquo">“</span>The study of rela­tion­ships with­in pub­lic rela­tions has become increas­ingly import­ant, with the Grunigs identi­fy­ing test­able vari­ables to meas­ure the qual­ity of organ­iz­a­tion­al rela­tion­ships.”<br><em>Source: <a href="https://doi.org/10.15173/JPC.V1I1.85" rel="noopener">Journal of Professional Communication</a></em> <span class="footnote_referrer"><span role="button" tabindex="0" onclick="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_3');" onkeypress="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_3');"><sup id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_65597_2_3" class="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text">3</sup></span><span id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text_65597_2_3" class="footnote_tooltip">Grunig, J. (2011). Public rela­tions excel­lence 2010. Journal of Professional <a alt="Free Communication PR Course" href="https://doctorspin.net/free-communication-pr-course/">Communication</a>, 1. <a href="https://doi.org/10.15173/JPC.V1I1.85" rel="noopener"><span class="footnote_url_wrap">https://​doi​.org/​1​0​.​1​5​1​7​3​/​J​P​C​.​V​1​I​1​.85</span></a></span></span><script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript" data-rocket-type="text/javascript"> jQuery('#footnote_plugin_tooltip_65597_2_3').tooltip({ tip: '#footnote_plugin_tooltip_text_65597_2_3', tipClass: 'footnote_tooltip', effect: 'fade', predelay: 0, fadeInSpeed: 200, delay: 400, fadeOutSpeed: 200, position: 'top center', relative: true, offset: [-7, 0], });</script></p>



<p data-block-type="core"><em>Learn more: <a href="https://doctorspin.net/four-models-of-public-relations/">The Four Models of Public Relations</a></em></p>



<h2  id="model-1-press-agentry-model" class="wp-block-heading" data-block-type="core">Model 1: Press Agentry Model</h2>



<p data-block-type="core">The press agentry mod­el, also known as the pub­li­city mod­el, emerged in the late 19<sup class="ordinal">th</sup> cen­tury and emphas­ises the power of per­sua­sion and manip­u­la­tion to shape pub­lic opinion.&nbsp;</p>



<p data-block-type="core">Historically, the pub­li­city mod­el of com­mu­nic­a­tion has been asso­ci­ated with the work of <a href="https://doctorspin.net/no-such-thing-as-bad-publicity/" data-type="post" data-id="66087">P.T. Barnum</a>, a&nbsp;show­man who used flam­boy­ant tac­tics to garner atten­tion for his cir­cus. In mod­ern busi­ness, the press agentry mod­el is often asso­ci­ated with pub­li­city stunts, press tours, and exag­ger­ated buzz around a&nbsp;product or event. <span class="footnote_referrer"><span role="button" tabindex="0" onclick="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_4');" onkeypress="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_4');"><sup id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_65597_2_4" class="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text">4</sup></span><span id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text_65597_2_4" class="footnote_tooltip">Silfwer, J. (2023, May 30).&nbsp;P.T. Barnum: “There’s No Such Thing as Bad Publicity.”&nbsp;Doctor Spin | The PR Blog. <a href="https://doctorspin.net/no-such-thing-as-bad-publicity/"><span class="footnote_url_wrap">https://​doc​tor​spin​.net/​n​o​-​s​u​c​h​-​t​h​i​n​g​-​a​s​-​b​a​d​-​p​u​b​l​i​c​i​ty/</span></a></span></span><script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript" data-rocket-type="text/javascript"> jQuery('#footnote_plugin_tooltip_65597_2_4').tooltip({ tip: '#footnote_plugin_tooltip_text_65597_2_4', tipClass: 'footnote_tooltip', effect: 'fade', predelay: 0, fadeInSpeed: 200, delay: 400, fadeOutSpeed: 200, position: 'top center', relative: true, offset: [-7, 0], });</script></p>



<p data-block-type="core">However, this one-way com­mu­nic­a­tion approach can be per­ceived as self-serving and may not always reflect an organ­isa­tion’s genu­ine val­ues or com­mit­ment to eth­ic­al conduct.</p>



<h2  id="model-2-public-information-model" class="wp-block-heading" data-block-type="core">Model 2: Public Information Model</h2>



<p data-block-type="core">The pub­lic inform­a­tion mod­el is a&nbsp;more trans­par­ent altern­at­ive to the press agentry mod­el. It focuses on dis­sem­in­at­ing accur­ate and object­ive information.</p>



<p data-block-type="core">This approach, often linked to the early 20<sup class="ordinal">th</sup>-cen­tury work of <a href="https://doctorspin.net/ivy-lee-first-press-release/" data-type="post" data-id="70806">Ive Lee</a>, a&nbsp;pion­eer in pub­lic rela­tions, seeks to foster trust and cred­ib­il­ity with key stake­hold­ers. Organisations that adopt the pub­lic inform­a­tion mod­el typ­ic­ally pri­or­it­ise provid­ing com­pre­hens­ive, unbiased data to the pub­lic, enabling stake­hold­ers to form their opin­ions based on reli­able facts. <span class="footnote_referrer"><span role="button" tabindex="0" onclick="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_5');" onkeypress="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_5');"><sup id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_65597_2_5" class="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text">5</sup></span><span id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text_65597_2_5" class="footnote_tooltip">Silfwer, J. (2012, February 19).&nbsp;Ivy Lee and the First <a alt="The Press Release Template" href="https://doctorspin.net/press-release-template/">Press Release</a>. Doctor Spin | The PR Blog. <a href="https://doctorspin.net/ivy-lee-first-press-release/"><span class="footnote_url_wrap">https://​doc​tor​spin​.net/​i​v​y​-​l​e​e​-​f​i​r​s​t​-​p​r​e​s​s​-​r​e​l​e​a​se/</span></a></span></span><script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript" data-rocket-type="text/javascript"> jQuery('#footnote_plugin_tooltip_65597_2_5').tooltip({ tip: '#footnote_plugin_tooltip_text_65597_2_5', tipClass: 'footnote_tooltip', effect: 'fade', predelay: 0, fadeInSpeed: 200, delay: 400, fadeOutSpeed: 200, position: 'top center', relative: true, offset: [-7, 0], });</script></p>



<p data-block-type="core">However, this mod­el is still rooted in one-way com­mu­nic­a­tion, and its effect­ive­ness is lim­ited by its lack of engage­ment with the audi­ence’s per­spect­ives and <a alt="Split Sessions: Recognition, Rewards, and Feedback" href="https://doctorspin.net/split-sessions/">feedback</a>.</p>



<h2  id="model-3-two-way-asymmetrical-model" class="wp-block-heading" data-block-type="core">Model 3: Two-Way Asymmetrical Model</h2>



<p data-block-type="core">The two-way asym­met­ric­al mod­el incor­por­ates per­sua­sion and feed­back to improve com­mu­nic­a­tions between an organ­isa­tion and its stake­hold­ers. This approach, which gained prom­in­ence in the mid-20<sup class="ordinal">th</sup> cen­tury, relies on research and under­stand­ing of the tar­get audi­ence to craft tailored mes­sages that res­on­ate with their val­ues and preferences.&nbsp;</p>



<p data-block-type="core">The primary goal of the two-way asym­met­ric­al mod­el is to influ­ence pub­lic opin­ion while sim­ul­tan­eously tak­ing audi­ence feed­back into account.&nbsp;</p>



<p data-block-type="core">Although this mod­el sig­ni­fic­antly improves on its pre­de­cessors in terms of audi­ence engage­ment, it still pri­or­it­ises the organ­isa­tion’s object­ives over the needs and con­cerns of its stakeholders.</p>



<h2  id="model-4-two-way-symmetrical-model" class="wp-block-heading" data-block-type="core">Model 4: Two-Way Symmetrical Model</h2>



<p data-block-type="core">Recognising the import­ance of fos­ter­ing mutu­ally bene­fi­cial rela­tion­ships with stake­hold­ers, Grunig and Hunt pro­posed the two-way sym­met­ric­al mod­el as the most eth­ic­al and prac­tic­al approach to pub­lic rela­tions. This mod­el emphas­ises open, trans­par­ent com­mu­nic­a­tion, wherein the organ­isa­tion and its stake­hold­ers engage in a&nbsp;dia­logue to under­stand each oth­er­’s per­spect­ives, val­ues, and con­cerns better.</p>



<p data-block-type="core">The two-way asym­met­ric­al mod­el pro­motes col­lab­or­a­tion, trust, and long-term rela­tion­ship build­ing by seek­ing a&nbsp;bal­ance between the interests of both parties.</p>



<p data-block-type="core">This approach is increas­ingly recog­nised as the gold stand­ard for pub­lic rela­tions prac­tice in today’s inter­con­nec­ted and glob­al­ised&nbsp;world.</p>



<h2  id="pr-models-for-every-situation" class="wp-block-heading" data-block-type="core">PR Models for Every Situation</h2>



<p data-block-type="core">Grunig and Hunt’s four mod­els of pub­lic rela­tions provide valu­able insights into the evol­u­tion of com­mu­nic­a­tion strategies and their implic­a­tions for mod­ern businesses.&nbsp;</p>



<p data-block-type="core">While each PR mod­el has <a alt="Never Use the Word &#039;Unique&#039; in Your PR writing" href="https://doctorspin.net/unique/">unique</a> strengths and lim­it­a­tions, the two-way sym­met­ric­al mod­el is the most eth­ic­al and prac­tic­al approach, fos­ter­ing genu­ine dia­logue and col­lab­or­a­tion between organ­isa­tions and their stakeholders.</p>



<p data-block-type="core">By integ­rat­ing the prin­ciples of the two-way asym­met­ric­al mod­el, busi­nesses can estab­lish a&nbsp;strong found­a­tion for last­ing suc­cess, trust, and cred­ib­il­ity in an increas­ingly com­pet­it­ive and inter­con­nec­ted&nbsp;world.</p>



<p data-block-type="core">It is import­ant to note that while the two-way sym­met­ric­al mod­el is widely con­sidered the most desir­able approach, the oth­er three mod­els may still have their place in spe­cif­ic contexts.&nbsp;</p>



<p data-block-type="core">For instance, press agentry (“the pub­li­city mod­el”) can gen­er­ate ini­tial interest in a&nbsp;product or event. In con­trast, the pub­lic inform­a­tion mod­el is appro­pri­ate for organ­isa­tions that primar­ily need to dis­sem­in­ate facts and fig­ures. Similarly, the two-way asym­met­ric­al mod­el can be effect­ive when a&nbsp;bal­ance between per­sua­sion and audi­ence engage­ment is required.</p>



<p data-block-type="core">To max­im­ise the effect­ive­ness of their pub­lic rela­tions strategies, organ­isa­tions should con­sider adopt­ing a&nbsp;hybrid approach that com­bines ele­ments of each mod­el as needed. This flex­ib­il­ity enables busi­nesses to adapt to their stake­hold­ers’ diverse and ever-chan­ging needs while main­tain­ing a&nbsp;sol­id com­mit­ment to hon­est and trans­par­ent communication.</p>



<h2  id="an-important-framework-for-pr" class="wp-block-heading" data-block-type="core">An Important Framework for&nbsp;PR</h2>



<p data-block-type="core">Ultimately, Grunig and Hunt’s four mod­els of pub­lic rela­tions serve as a&nbsp;valu­able PR the­ory for under­stand­ing the vari­ous approaches to com­mu­nic­a­tion and their poten­tial impact on an organ­isa­tion’s repu­ta­tion and relationships.&nbsp;</p>



<p data-block-type="core">By recog­nising the strengths and lim­it­a­tions of each PR mod­el, busi­nesses can make informed decisions about the most appro­pri­ate strategies for their unique cir­cum­stances. This fosters a&nbsp;cul­ture of open­ness, col­lab­or­a­tion, and mutu­al under­stand­ing with pub­lics, stake­hold­ers, and influ­en­cers. <span class="footnote_referrer"><span role="button" tabindex="0" onclick="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_6');" onkeypress="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_6');"><sup id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_65597_2_6" class="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text">6</sup></span><span id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text_65597_2_6" class="footnote_tooltip">Grunig, J. E., &amp;&nbsp;Hunt, T. (1984). Managing pub­lic rela­tions. Holt, Rinehart, and Winston.</span></span><script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript" data-rocket-type="text/javascript"> jQuery('#footnote_plugin_tooltip_65597_2_6').tooltip({ tip: '#footnote_plugin_tooltip_text_65597_2_6', tipClass: 'footnote_tooltip', effect: 'fade', predelay: 0, fadeInSpeed: 200, delay: 400, fadeOutSpeed: 200, position: 'top center', relative: true, offset: [-7, 0], });</script> <span class="footnote_referrer"><span role="button" tabindex="0" onclick="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_7');" onkeypress="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_7');"><sup id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_65597_2_7" class="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text">7</sup></span><span id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text_65597_2_7" class="footnote_tooltip">Ledingham, J. A., &amp;&nbsp;Bruning, S. D. (Eds.). (2000). Public rela­tions as rela­tion­ship man­age­ment: A&nbsp;rela­tion­al approach to the study and prac­tice of pub­lic rela­tions. Routledge.</span></span><script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript" data-rocket-type="text/javascript"> jQuery('#footnote_plugin_tooltip_65597_2_7').tooltip({ tip: '#footnote_plugin_tooltip_text_65597_2_7', tipClass: 'footnote_tooltip', effect: 'fade', predelay: 0, fadeInSpeed: 200, delay: 400, fadeOutSpeed: 200, position: 'top center', relative: true, offset: [-7, 0], });</script> <span class="footnote_referrer"><span role="button" tabindex="0" onclick="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_8');" onkeypress="footnote_moveToReference_65597_2('footnote_plugin_reference_65597_2_8');"><sup id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_65597_2_8" class="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text">8</sup></span><span id="footnote_plugin_tooltip_text_65597_2_8" class="footnote_tooltip">Cutlip, S. <style></style>
<div class="td-block-title-wrap"><h4 class="td-block-title"><span>The Cover Photo</span></h4></div></div><div class="wpb_wrapper wpb_text_column td_block_wrap td_block_wrap vc_column_text tdi_79  tagdiv-type td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_79" >

<div class="td-fix-index"><p>The cover photo isn't related to public relations obviously; it's just a photo of mine. Think of it as a 'decorative diversion', a subtle reminder that it's good to have hobbies outside work.</p>
</div></div><div class="tdm_block td_block_wrap tdm_block_inline_text tdi_80 td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_80" >

<div class="tdi_85_rand_style td-element-style"><style></style></div><div class="tdb-block-inner td-fix-index"><div class="tdb-cf-add-txt">Shareable:</div><div class="tdb-sacff-txt">Four models to unlock <a alt="My TEDx PR Talk (200K+ Views): A Recipe for PR Success" href="https://doctorspin.net/tedx-pr-talk/">PR success</a>.</div></div></div> <!-- ./block --><div class="td_block_wrap tdb_single_custom_field tdi_86 tdb-sacff-type-text td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_86" >


<div class="tdi_86_rand_style td-element-style"><style></style></div><div class="tdb-block-inner td-fix-index"><div class="tdb-cf-add-txt">Shareable:</div><div class="tdb-sacff-txt">The researchers concluded that the two-way symmetrical model is the most effective way to practice public relations.</div></div></div> <!-- ./block --><div class="td_block_wrap tdb_single_custom_field tdi_87 tdb-sacff-type-text td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_87" >


<div class="tdi_87_rand_style td-element-style"><style></style></div><div class="tdb-block-inner td-fix-index"><div class="tdb-cf-add-txt">Shareable:</div><div class="tdb-sacff-txt">In the Excellence study, James Grunig and Todd Hunt (1984) developed the most widely cited PR model in academic circles.</div></div></div> <!-- ./block --><div class="td_block_wrap tdb_single_custom_field tdi_88 tdb-sacff-type-text td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_88" >


<div class="tdi_88_rand_style td-element-style"><style></style></div><div class="tdb-block-inner td-fix-index"><div class="tdb-cf-add-txt">Shareable:</div><div class="tdb-sacff-txt">The press agentry model, also known as the <a alt="Public Relations vs Publicity" href="https://doctorspin.net/public-relations-vs-publicity/">publicity</a> model, emerged in the late 19th century and emphasises the power of persuasion and manipulation to shape public opinion.</div></div></div> <!-- ./block --><div class="td_block_wrap tdb_single_custom_field tdi_89 tdb-sacff-type-text td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_89" >


<div class="tdi_89_rand_style td-element-style"><style></style></div><div class="tdb-block-inner td-fix-index"><div class="tdb-cf-add-txt">Shareable:</div><div class="tdb-sacff-txt">As a more transparent alternative to the press agentry model, the public information model focuses on disseminating accurate and objective information. </div></div></div> <!-- ./block --><div class="td_block_wrap tdb_single_custom_field tdi_90 tdb-sacff-type-text td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_90" >


<div class="tdi_90_rand_style td-element-style"><style></style></div><div class="tdb-block-inner td-fix-index"><div class="tdb-cf-add-txt">Shareable:</div><div class="tdb-sacff-txt">The primary goal of the two-way asymmetrical model is to influence public opinion while simultaneously taking audience feedback into account. </div></div></div> <!-- ./block --><div class="td_block_wrap tdb_single_custom_field tdi_91 tdb-sacff-type-text td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_91" >


<div class="tdi_91_rand_style td-element-style"><style></style></div><div class="tdb-block-inner td-fix-index"><div class="tdb-cf-add-txt">Shareable:</div><div class="tdb-sacff-txt">By seeking a balance between the interests of both parties, the two-way asymmetrical model promotes collaboration, trust, and long-term relationship building. </div></div></div> <!-- ./block --><div class="td_block_wrap tdb_single_custom_field tdi_92 tdb-sacff-type-text td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_92" >


<div class="tdi_92_rand_style td-element-style"><style></style></div><div class="tdb-block-inner td-fix-index"><div class="tdb-cf-add-txt">Shareable:</div><div class="tdb-sacff-txt">Grunig and Hunt’s four models of public relations provide valuable insights into the evolution of communication strategies and their implications for modern businesses. </div></div></div> <!-- ./block --><div class="td_block_wrap tdb_single_custom_field tdi_93 tdb-sacff-type-text td-pb-border-top td_block_template_1"  data-td-block-uid="tdi_93" >


<div class="tdi_93_rand_style td-element-style"><style></style></div><div class="tdb-block-inner td-fix-index"><div class="tdb-cf-add-txt">Shareable:</div><div class="tdb-sacff-txt">It is important to note that while the two-way symmetrical model is widely considered the most desirable approach, the other three models may still have their place in specific contexts. </div></div></div> <!-- ./block --><div class="wpb_wrapper td_block_empty_space td_block_wrap vc_empty_space tdi_95 "  style="height: 50px"></div></div></div><div class="vc_column tdi_97  wpb_column vc_column_container tdc-column td-pb-span3">
<div class="wpb_wrapper" ></div></div></div></div><div id="tdi_98" class="tdc-row stretch_row_content td-stretch-content"><div class="vc_row tdi_99  wpb_row td-pb-row" >

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript" data-rocket-type="text/javascript">// Redirect Contact Form 7 to different thank-you pages based on form ID
document.addEventListener('wpcf7mailsent', function(event) {
    var formID = event.detail.contactFormId;

    // Redirect based on form ID
    switch (formID) {
        case '6707442':  // Replace with the ID of the Contact form
            window.location.href = 'https://doctorspin.net/thank-you-contact/';
            break;
        case '229f3cd':  // Replace with the ID of the Footer form
            window.location.href = 'https://doctorspin.net/thank-you-contact/';
            break;
        case '6c8169f': // Replace with the ID of the Challenge form
            window.location.href = 'https://doctorspin.net/thank-you/';
            break;
        default:
            window.location.href = 'https://doctorspin.net/thank-you-contact/';  // Fallback if no match
            break;
    }
}, false);
</script><script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">requestAnimationFrame(() => document.body.classList.add( "stk--anim-init" ))</script><script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript" data-rocket-type="text/javascript" data-rocket-src="https://u2a6h5f3.delivery.rocketcdn.me/wp-includes/js/jquery/ui/core.min.js?ver=1.13.3" id="jquery-ui-core-js" data-rocket-defer defer></script>

                /* global jQuery:{} */
                jQuery().ready(function () {

                    let td_show = "[show]";
                    let td_hide = "[hide]";
                    let initial_position = true;
                    if (initial_position === false) {
                        jQuery("#panel").slideUp("slow");
                    }

                    jQuery("#flip").click(function() {
                        jQuery("#panel").slideToggle("slow", function() {
                            const isVisible = jQuery("#panel").is(":visible");
                            jQuery("#flip").text(isVisible ? td_hide : td_show);
                        });
                    });

                    let uid = 'tdi_73',
                        $blockObj = jQuery('.tdi_73');

                    let tdbTOCItem = new tdbTOC.item();
                    // block uid
                    tdbTOCItem.uid = uid;
                    // block object
                    tdbTOCItem.blockObj = $blockObj;

                    // smooth scroll
                    tdbTOCItem.smoothScroll = '1';
                    tdbTOCItem.scrollOffset = '20';
                    tdbTOCItem.scrollDuration = '500';
                    tdbTOCItem.activeHighlight = '1';

                    tdbTOC.addItem(tdbTOCItem);

                });
            
            

            
                jQuery(window).on( 'load', function () {
                    var block = jQuery('.tdi_81'),
                        blockClass = '.tdi_81',
                        blockInner = block.find('.tdb-block-inner'),
                        blockOffsetLeft;

                    if( block.find('audio').length > 0 ) {
                        jQuery(blockClass + ' audio').mediaelementplayer();
                    }

                    if( block.hasClass('tdb-sfi-stretch') ) {
                        jQuery(window).resize(function () {
                            blockOffsetLeft = block.offset().left;

                            if( block.hasClass('tdb-sfi-stretch-left') ) {
                                blockInner.css('margin-left', -blockOffsetLeft + 'px');
                            } else {
                                blockInner.css('margin-right', -(jQuery(window).width() - (blockOffsetLeft + block.outerWidth())) + 'px');
                            }
                        });
                        jQuery(window).resize();
                    }

                    setTimeout(function () {
                        block.css('opacity', 1);
                    }, 500);
                });
            
            
</script>
